The mp-w345 units W4-CC-7, W4-PL-1 and W5-074-b may also claim a number, so the lead should re-check for a collision at merge.; Immutability trigger (changes vs the 0108 pattern): a DELETE is allowed under mp.version_erasure = 'on', or when the parent procedures row is already gone, which is how the ON DELETE CASCADE from deleting a procedure is recognised. 0108 reset the setting in an AFTER DELETE trigger on the parent. That would not work here because the cascade is an RI action whose ordering against user triggers is not reliable. A direct delete of one version while the procedure exists raises.; procedure_tags has a composite foreign key (procedure_id, version) to procedure_versions with ON DELETE CASCADE, so a tag cannot point at a missing version. It is CASCADE rather than NO ACTION because NO ACTION could fail when versions are removed before tags during the parent cascade. Tags also have an updated_at column the spec did not list.; The migration adds checks the spec did not list: the id shape, non-empty org_id and owner_user_id, source_run_id <= 128, latest_version >= 1, jsonb_typeof(body) = 'object', step_count 1..100, and a procedure_id index (procedures_owner_idx) on (org_id, owner_user_id, created_at DESC, id DESC) for keyset listing. It is written to be re-run at every boot, since docker-entrypoint.sh re-applies every *.up.sql.; Validate also enforces, beyond the spec: closed key sets for the top level, steps and slots; slot names unique, matching ^[A-Za-z0-9_.-]{1,64}$ and kind text or secret; each slot used by exactly one typed step; slot allowed only on type and type_semantic steps; a slot's step number must match the step's if both are present; JSON depth <= 32; numbers normalised to integers or floats so the digest does not depend on number spelling; and the redaction-marker check is case-insensitive and also covers object keys. The text rule applies only to a typed action's own top-level text, because locator.text is a legitimate key.; Digest is sha256 of the canonical body: map keys sorted by encoding/json, HTML escaping off, no trailing newline. The canonical bytes are what gets stored, so the digest always describes the stored body. Also added: Canonicalize and DigestOf, so a reader can re-verify a stored version.; The store takes an Owner{OrgID, UserID} value in every method; the handler in 3b must fill both from verifiedOrganizationID and verifiedActorID. A procedure that is not the caller's answers ErrNotFound. The store adds ErrVersionLimit (200 versions) and ErrTagLimit (32 tags) as runaway guards, which the spec did not ask for. |

The new internal/api/procedures.go maps the eight routes onto the Q4-E4.2-3a store (create, list, get, version read, add version, set and delete tag, delete procedure). Identity comes only from the verified token (no person id is 403), store validation refusals are 400 with the closed code, every read and write writes a content-free registry_audit_log row, and request bodies are capped at 256 KiB via MaxBytesReader. main.go wires it beside the skill bundle handler, and compose plus the flag registry carry the switch. Nothing was compiled or tested (CODE ONLY); procedures.go passes gofmt -l, and main.go already failed gofmt -l at HEAD on the skillbundle import order, which I left alone. | - Deviations: Edited docker-compose.yml and flag-registry.json, which the unit's file list does not name: the hard rules require a new Model Plane compose switch to be in both files in the same change.; main.go change is two lines (a comment plus the NewProceduresHandler call) rather than the single line the spec named.; Response shapes are not fixed by the spec and were chosen here: create 201 {procedure_id, version, digest}; list 200 {procedures, next_after_id (omitted on the last page)}; get 200 {id, name, description, source_run_id, latest_version, created_at, updated_at, latest:{version, digest, step_count, slot_names, created_at}, tags:[{tag, version}]}; version read 200 {procedure_id, version, digest, step_count, slot_names, created_at, procedure:<canonical body>}; add version 201; set tag 200 {procedure_id, tag, version}; both deletes 204. Errors use the typed envelope with codes procedures_disabled (404), procedure_not_found (404), procedure_version_limit and procedure_tag_limit (409), invalid_request, invalid_limit, invalid_after_id, invalid_version (400), procedure_body_too_large (413), authentication_required (401), forbidden (403), database_unavailable (500/503), procedure_digest_mismatch (500), plus the store's validation codes (400).; Audit rows go to registry_audit_log with entity_kind 'procedure' and need no migration (the table has no action CHECK). A read whose audit insert fails answers 500 with no body. A write's audit runs after the store commits, because the store owns its own transaction, so a failed audit is logged rather than returned and the write and its audit are not atomic.; A version read re-canonicalises the stored JSONB body with procedures.Canonicalize and compares the digest with the recorded one; a mismatch answers 500 procedure_digest_mismatch instead of the body.; The 256 KiB request cap covers the whole request, so the envelope (name, description, source_run_id) shares it with the body; a body near the cap cannot also carry metadata.; No ZDR check was added in capability-core, per the spec's ZDR line. authz.AuthorizeHTTP already refuses every non-read method for a ZDR principal before the handler runs, so ZDR writes never reach it; ZDR reads are not refused here. |

Owner decisions taken at the recommended option (dark code):

- **Q4-E4.1-3b**: B2(a): index first; export is a read, so no action-registry change
- **Q4-E4.1-7a**: E4.1-7: delete the Go presets (plan recommendation), rename the idea to 'saved job'
- **Q4-E4.3-055c1**: B4: crawls run only through the gateway under the user token after a person approves; the tool never starts a crawl (recommended user-token route, no Control Plane PR)
- **Q4-062b-sse**: Record-keeping around the in-stream pipeline; no detached execution (bearer refresh not designed)
- **Q4-E4.2-3a**: B2(b): capability-core owns the saved-recipe store with its own migration. EP-11 skill_versions is not the store (prompt-text kind enum skill/template/preset, trigger-matched prompt injection, no tags); only its immutability/digest pattern is reused
- **Q4-E4.2-3b**: B2(b) capability-core store; owner-only scope
- **Q4-E4.1-6b**: Update path is a dedicated PUT .../definition (config.definition only), not a generic config write
- **Q4-E4.3-043c1**: Version carries url_patterns (EP-11 design, W4-ORCH2 recommendation); digest-neutral for existing rows

Ledger notes (move into the decision ledger when a unit is proven):

- **Q4-E4.1-3b**: Q4-E4.1-3b is built, dark by the edge flag only. The gateway route `GET /api/ingestions/jobs/{id}/results/export` withholds ZDR orgs before any upstream call and takes the org from the session token. It sends no content on 501 or 403, and it wraps a csv or jsonl page (max 100 rows, 10 MiB cap) as JSON for the SPA. `to_results_view` now carries `kind`. The Innhenting results view shows the page-index caption and CSV / JSON Lines buttons that export the page on screen. There is no new AppState flag, no action-registry row and no golden change. Quarry docs/openapi.yaml is untouched because the gateway route is not a Quarry public route.
- **Q4-E4.1-7a**: E4.1-7 (Q4-E4.1-7a): the Go job presets are gone. presets.go and presets_test.go are deleted, GET /v1/presets and GET /v1/presets/{name} are no longer mounted on quarry-control, and the deprecated stealth alias went with them. POST /v1/jobs refuses a non-empty preset with 400 BAD_REQUEST ("preset was removed; send policy") rather than resolving or silently ignoring it, and an explicit policy still takes precedence over the default. The word "preset" keeps only its cadence meaning (hourly, daily, weekly on change-monitor sources and schedules) and the Rust quarry_core bundles; a named, reusable set of job options is a saved job definition (E4.1-6).
- **Q4-E4.3-055c1**: Q4-E4.3-055c1 is fully in the tree. `knowledge.crawl_plan` is a read-only planner in execution-core. It calls `/v1/map` (`map:read`) with limit 50 and search set to the goal, then returns the pages, the validated scope and one fenced `crawl-plan` v1 JSON block. The scope caps match the gateway's `crawlSiteInput`: depth 1..=10, at most 100 patterns of 512 characters each, `max_pages` clamped to 1..=`MAX_CRAWL_PAGES`. Goal and titles are capped and stripped of backticks and newlines. It is not a durable tool, so ZDR runs keep it. It sits behind the existing `EXECUTION_CORE_WEB_CRAWL_TOOLS` switch (default off) and org `web.mode=off`. It binds to `cap.web.map` (seed row is Q4-E4.3-055c2) and is classified `ExternalWeb` in `mp-content-safety` and session-core. It starts no job, which is the B4 user-token route.
- **Q4-062b-sse**: Q4-062b-sse: the chat deep-research branch in model-gateway src/sse.rs begins a research run record before any search when MODEL_GATEWAY_RESEARCH_RUNS is on (helpers begin_research_run, finish_research_run, ResearchRunProgress, research_finish and research_partial; the spec's free research_runs::begin does not exist, so the ResearchRunsClient method is wrapped). An active-run conflict ends the turn with a research_run_active error frame and terminalizes the chat run. Status maps Completed to completed, Cancelled to cancelled, Deadline, BudgetUsd, CostUnpriced and NoEvidence to stopped, and an audit failure to failed. A non-ZDR run keeps its partial result and report id; a ZDR run keeps neither. research_budget (normalize.rs; InvokeRequest.research_budget) clamps usd_ceiling to 0.01 to 25 and drops integer caps below 1. Open items for the lead: (1) a run whose synthesis failed (report None) reports completed, per the spec, while the pipeline ledger note asked for failed; (2) the verevonv3 SPA and BFF do not send research_budget yet (not in this unit's files); (3) the session-core flag-registry purpose text and the compose comment for RESEARCH_RUNS_ENABLED still say nothing starts a run before the gateway slice; (4) Session Core refuses writes from a ZDR-credential token, so those turns run with no record and the turn continues. Compile focus: the tuple coercions in the emit_prepared_failure arguments (same shape as the existing audit branch), the BTreeMap collect in research_partial, and the Send bounds of the tokio::spawn in ResearchRunProgress::report.
- **Q4-E4.2-3a**: Q4-E4.2-3a built (code only, uncompiled, no tests written): capability-core migration 0039_procedures (+ down) and internal/procedures {procedure.go, store.go}. Tables procedures, procedure_versions (immutable, digest-pinned) and procedure_tags; scope is 'user' only (sharing is ADR 8, not built). The immutability trigger allows a delete only under mp.version_erasure = 'on' or when the parent row is already gone (the cascade). Validate accepts only BrowserProcedure v2 (version 2, 1..100 steps, <= 256 KiB, closed 12-action vocabulary). It refuses any approval_grant_id key, any non-empty text on a typed step, a typed step without a declared slot, and any "[redacted" marker. The store keeps the canonical body and its sha256; every query is scoped by org id and owner user id, which the caller takes from the verified token. No switch in this unit: the PROCEDURES_API gate comes in 3b, and nothing reads the tables until then. Follow-ups: session-core gdpr.rs does not yet erase procedures by owner or org (the migration comment says the per-user erasure path is not built). The migration number 0039 should be re-checked against mp-w345 claims (W4-CC-7, W4-PL-1, W5-074-b) at merge.
- **Q4-E4.2-3b**: B2(b) saved-procedure HTTP surface built dark (Q4-E4.2-3b): /api/v1/procedures routes over capability-core internal/procedures, owner-only, 404 procedures_disabled while PROCEDURES_API is off, content-free registry_audit_log rows with entity_kind 'procedure'. Write audits are best-effort after commit and not atomic with the store transaction; read audits fail closed. Compose default false and a flags registry entry were added in the same change. Cross-unit flag for Q4-E4.2-3d: its GET /v1/recipes/:id/versions is described as listing "via the capability-core summary", but 3b has no version-list route, only GET /api/v1/procedures/{id} (summary plus tags), so the gateway should map that GET to the summary or a list route must be added.
- **Q4-E4.1-6b**: Q4-E4.1-6b (quarry-control, branch quarry-w45): UpdateConfigByOrg on SourcesStore with memSources and pg implementations, GET /v1/sources/{id}, and PUT /v1/sources/{id}/definition with a structural definition check (version 1, job_kind equal to the source kind, options object, 16 KiB) were already in the tree from the interrupted run; the re-run verified them complete and made no edits. The new paths sit outside the orchestrator-only prefixes, so the edge secret verifies them. No migration, switch, proto or golden change. The public edge routes that will call these paths are a separate unit and will need openapi.yaml entries there; the control routes themselves are not public. Nothing was compiled or tested in this run.
- **Q4-E4.3-043a**: Q4-E4.3-043a (E4.3 slice 1, EXECUTION_CORE_WEB_GUIDANCE, default off): execution-core agent.rs adds a prompt-only public-web snippet (SNIPPET_WEB, built from the offered set) and longer descriptions for web_search, web_fetch, web.search and web.read in base_tool_defs. No parameter schema, dispatch, persistence or ZDR path changes, and with the switch off the prompt and tool list are byte-identical. The switch is process-level and must not change between a park and its resume. It has no flag-registry or compose row, matching the routing switch.
- **Q4-E4.3-043c1**: Q4-E4.3-043c1 built (storage half of E4.3 skill url_patterns): proto fields AgentSkill 23, UpsertAgentSkillRequest 16, AgentSkillVersion 14; session-core migration 0116 adds agent_skills.url_patterns and makes agent_skill_version_body carry the key only when non-empty, so all existing digests and versions stay byte-identical; upsert validates and writes it, ListAgentSkills returns it, skill version reads expose it. No switch (empty list is inert); matching is Q4-E4.3-043c2. Merge notes: the mp-w345 checkout already has 0115_browser_help_timed_out_failure_code and 0116_agent_skills_teach_origin, so migration numbers in this tree (0115_research_runs, 0116_url_patterns) must be renumbered at merge (no logical conflict: the teach_origin file does not touch the body function). Known gap: RESTORE_SKILL_SQL / the revision snapshot do not carry url_patterns, so undoing a skill delete restores it without patterns. Add a test that a skill with patterns gets a version whose body has url_patterns and one without keeps the old digest; extend digest_field_list_is_pinned to pin the conditional key against 0116.

| Q4-E4.3-043c2 | E4.3 Track A F-t2-043 slice 3 matching half (plan 3.4 E4.3 Scope (3): matched on browser.observe inside the 8000-character skill budget) | built | Added the new module runtime_loop/skill_url.rs and hooked it into the Do loop's execute_step_inner in runtime_loop/mod.rs. When EXECUTION_CORE_SKILL_URL_MATCH is the literal "true", a successful browser.observe has its page_url read from the observation JSON before the output is screened. After the page text is screened, quarantined and rendered, matching site skills are appended as `\n\n[site skill]\n<blocks>`, so the guidance is never screened as page content. Matching is pure and follows the closed grammar: lowercase host or `*.host`, an optional /path-prefix, an optional trailing `*`, http/https only, and query/fragment/port/credentials ignored. select_for_url keeps enabled, accepted, kind ''/'skill' skills with url_patterns and content, longest matching pattern first, at most 2. The 8000-char budget uses skill_budget::fit_skill_blocks. Skills are listed once per run (org, run) through session_channel/session_bearer with enabled_only:true, cached 5 min with a 256-run cap, and each (skill id, version) is injected once per run. Every failure returns the output unchanged, and there is a defensive !zdr check. The switch is added to the execution-core env in the Model Plane compose (default false) and to the flag-registry "unregistered" list in the same change. | - Deviations: lib.rs not changed: the module is declared in runtime_loop/mod.rs next to skill_budget, and runtime_loop is already declared in lib.rs, so no lib.rs edit was needed.; The hook is after screening/quarantine/render, applied to the final StepOutcome.output (after parking and the edit note), not literally between `let output = exec.output;` and the provenance assess. The page_url is read at the spec'd spot, before the output moves into screening; the append happens at the end so the skill text is outside the screened and labelled page content.; review_state accepted is implemented as empty-or-'accepted', matching reattach_skill and fetch_skill_context in the same crate (older rows have an empty review_state).; `*.host` matches subdomains only (not the bare host) and requires at least two labels after `*.`, so `*.com` matches nothing; `host*` with no path is outside the grammar and matches nothing. A path without `*` is a segment-boundary prefix; with a trailing `*` it is a plain text prefix.; Only skills with non-empty url_patterns are kept in the per-run cache. A failed skill read is not cached, so the next observe retries.; Dedupe marks only the blocks that survive the budget fit. Both the 5-minute cache and the injected set live in one process-wide mutex map keyed by (org_id, run_id), with expired-first then oldest eviction at 256 runs. There is no explicit run-end cleanup; the TTL and the cap bound it. |

Compile fix, Quarry-v2 Rust workspace: green. 0 fixes.

Compile fix, Frontend Plane verevonv3 (SPA + BFF gateway): green. 0 fixes. Unresolved: No gateway (apps/gateway) files changed in the batch, so cargo check was not run.; global.css is not type-checked (CSS only).

Compile fix, Go modules, proto bindings, SQL migrations, JSON/YAML/compose (everything outsid: green. 7 fixes.

Compile fix, Model Plane Rust workspace (apps/Model Plane/rust) and protos: green. 0 fixes.

Owner decisions taken at the recommended option (dark code):

- **Q4-E4.3-055c2**: Share cap.web.map (read, low) rather than a new capability
- **Q4-E4.3-055c4**: B4 user-token route: the approval click calls existing gateway actions under the user's own token; no new action id
- **Q4-E4.1-6c**: Saved definitions are validated at the edge with the existing crawl/scrape validators; control only checks structure
- **Q4-E4.1-6d**: target_ref = source id (plan wording); only crawl definitions are schedulable because ScrapeJobWF reads url+ingest only
- **Q4-E4.2-3c**: gRPC (not HTTP) read for execution-core, matching the skill-bundle precedent and its existing capability:read token
- **Q4-E4.2-3d**: proxy through model-gateway like skills, so the BFF talks to one Model Plane HTTP origin
- **Q4-E4.3-043c2**: Match only in the Do loop browser.observe; browser_agent sub-agent planner matching is left out (planner_view cannot carry skills without a PlannerContext change)
- **Q4-E4.3-043d1**: B2(c): integration-corev2 serves the provider operation catalog (recommended option)

Ledger notes (move into the decision ledger when a unit is proven):

- **Q4-E4.3-055c2**: Q4-E4.3-055c2 is built on quarry-w45 and uncommitted. knowledge.crawl_plan is seeded as read with no approval floor under the existing cap.web.map by capability-core migration 0040, with cap.web.map's tool_family extended and reverted by the down file. There is no new capability, switch or availability change. Open item for the ledger: no code in this tree attests cap.web.* (the 0031 header describes an attestation that is not implemented here), so cap.web.map stays health_not_attested until an attestation path lands under E4.3. Migration 0040 must be re-checked against the mp-w345 migrations at merge.
- **Q4-E4.3-055c4**: Q4-E4.3-055c4 built (code only, uncompiled): CrawlPagePicker plan mode plus the new chat CrawlPlanFence component, dark behind VITE_CRAWL_PLAN_CARD. The model proposes through a closed crawl-plan fence, and the person approves by clicking either 'Crawl N valgte' (crawlSelectedPages batch) or 'Crawl med dette omfanget' (startCrawl, the knowledge.crawl_site contract). Both calls run under the user's own token, with orgId from getSessionContext and ingest from resolveCrawlIngest. Typed refusals such as ZDR 403 and approval-required are shown as text. No new action id, so no admission golden changes. The component is not yet mounted in the chat markdown renderer.
- **Q4-E4.1-6c**: Q4-E4.1-6c: the edge now validates saved job definitions at source create and adds `GET /v1/sources/:id` and `PUT /v1/sources/:id/definition`, all behind QUARRY_EDGE__SAVED_JOBS (default off, 501 when off). Validation uses saved_job::check, i.e. the live crawl and scrape validators; control still checks structure only. The org comes from the verified claims, and the web policy is applied to the source URL on create and on a definition set (not on a clear). A denied URL is refused at save time. Nothing was compiled or run. The openapi route-coverage test stays red until Q4-E4.1-6e documents `PUT /v1/sources/{id}/definition`; `route_registrations` is re-pinned from 130 to 131. The docs/openapi.yaml entries for the new GET and PUT are Q4-E4.1-6e's job. `get_source` and `put_source_definition` need tests (CODE ONLY rule).
- **Q4-E4.1-6d**: Q4-E4.1-6d (quarry-w45, code only, not compiled): POST /v1/schedules takes source_id for an executable schedule; target_ref is the source id and the config is rebuilt from the saved crawl definition at create time. Gap check: schedule_routes.rs had no source_id before this unit, while saved_job.rs (6a) and the source read and definition routes (6c) were already in the tree. Open for later units: the gateway schedule create (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/ingestions/schedules.rs) builds its own body from name, kind, cron, schedule_at, timezone and URL fields and does not forward source_id, so the SPA cannot reach this path yet. docs/SCHEDULES.md (refusal-reason list near line 64) does not yet list source_id or the new reasons.
- **Q4-E4.1-6e**: E4.1-6e documents the saved job definition routes built in 6a to 6d: openapi (GET and PUT on /v1/sources/{source_id}, config.definition on POST /v1/sources, source_id on POST /v1/schedules, SavedJobDefinition and SourceDefinitionRequest), CONTRACTS ("Saved job definitions (E4.1-6)"), and RUNBOOKS ("Saved job definitions: enable, disable and the refusals", plus a QUARRY_EDGE__SAVED_JOBS row in Schedules go-live). QUARRY_EDGE__SAVED_JOBS stays default off. Going live waits for X-9 and for QUARRY_SCHEDULED_JOBS_ENABLED on control. The docs record that a scrape definition with no options is schedulable and one with options is refused as not_schedulable.
- **Q4-E4.2-3c**: Q4-E4.2-3c (capability-core gRPC GetProcedureVersion): the procedures store is now readable by execution-core over gRPC, as a read only, with the same PROCEDURES_API switch as the HTTP routes (default off, so it is dark). A user caller reads only its own procedures. A service caller needs capability:read and must name for_user_id, which only execution-core may assert, as with user_id on EvaluatePolicy. The served body is the canonical form of the stored body and is checked against its digest before it is served. The read writes no audit row, which differs from the HTTP read routes. The explicit method rule covers GetProcedureVersion only. D2-383 stays open for ListSkillBundles, GetSkillBundleManifest and GetSkillBlob.
- **Q4-E4.2-3d**: Q4-E4.2-3d built (code only, not compiled): nine /v1/recipes proxies in apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/http_routes.rs over capability-core /api/v1/procedures, dark through PROCEDURES_API with no gateway switch. GET /v1/recipes/:id/versions maps to the procedure summary, which closes the Q4-E4.2-3b cross-unit flag. Path ids are checked as plain keys and tags as ^[a-z0-9._-]{1,32}$ minus dot segments; bodies are capped at 256 KiB; no org or user identity is read from the request or body. Open: five space-surface rows for the non-GET routes must be added and re-pinned before zdr_posture every_non_get_route_is_classified passes; apps/Model Plane/api/openapi.yaml is not updated.
- **Q4-E4.3-043c2**: Q4-E4.3-043c2: execution-core now matches org skills' url_patterns on a Do-loop browser.observe (runtime_loop/skill_url.rs), behind EXECUTION_CORE_SKILL_URL_MATCH (literal true, default off, registered in the flag registry and compose). Up to 2 skills, longest matching pattern first, inside the shared 8000-char skill budget. The text is appended as `[site skill]` after the screened/rendered page output, so it is advisory, never screened as page content, and grants no tool or permission. Each (skill id, version) is injected once per run; the per-run skill list is cached for 5 minutes (256 runs max). Any failure leaves the observation unchanged; a defensive !zdr check is in place. Left out by decision: browser_agent sub-agent planner matching (planner_view cannot carry skills without a PlannerContext change).
- **Q4-E4.3-056c**: E4.3-056c built in integration-corev2 on branch quarry-w45 (uncommitted). Notion page.content (alias notion.page.content) is a GET of /v1/blocks/{pageId}/children, capability content.read, sensitive, not approval-gated, dark by design with no switch. Reads write no action receipt, and the audit metadata carries only the operation name, so no response body is stored by this service (ZDR holds). Agents cannot call it yet: the Model Plane execution-core catalog and the mp-contracts table do not list it, which is a Model Plane unit outside this unit's planes. The read golden test covers microsoft and google only, so a notion row in provider-read-operations.json would also need goldenProviders extended in read_operations_golden_test.go.
- **Q4-E4.3-043d1**: Q4-E4.3-043d1 built. integration-corev2 serves GET /api/v1/actions/catalog (internal/api/actions_catalog.go): a read-only, versioned catalog of 49 operations, with kind from actionRequiresApproval and capability and sensitive from requiredCapabilityForOperation computed per request, unmapped rows dropped, etag = SHA-256 of the document, 304 on a matching If-None-Match. No switch (read-only metadata), ZDR-neutral, no org or connection data. execution-core (043d2) must treat it as an addition-only hint that never lowers a write. Frozen-contract section 8 was added to docs/actions-surface-operations.md. Follow-up for the Model Plane owner: execution-core's static CATALOG summary for slack message.send says params.channel, but the executor reads body.channel.

Dark: no switch and no reader yet; evaluation is 1b and edge wiring is 1c behind QUARRY_EDGE__STEP_EXPECT (default off). Nothing is persisted under ZDR.
- **Q4-E4.2-1b**: Q4-E4.2-1b built in quarry-runtime. step_expect.rs holds the pure evaluator and the QUARRY_EDGE__STEP_EXPECT reader. ObservationContext.pending_expect is taken at the top of execute, so an early return never leaks it to the next step. The verdict is folded into ActionOutcome by ObservationRunner::apply_expectation (Met turns Unknown into Verified expect_satisfied; NotMet gives Failed expect_not_met with "unmet conditions: i,j" over any base; Unevaluable keeps the base and sets detail "expect_unevaluable: <reason>" when the base is Unknown). ProofBundle.expectation carries the ExpectationReport. Page text is built in memory only (strip_tags, 256 KiB cap), never stored, and a withheld otp_denied page offers no snapshot or text. The verdict carries indexes and closed tokens only. Not compiled or tested. Unit 1c (the edge) must set ctx.pending_expect while the switch is on.
- **Q4-E4.2-1c**: Q4-E4.2-1c (code only, not compiled or tested): StepBody accepts an optional expect. step() and delegate_step() refuse it with 400 unsupported_option while QUARRY_EDGE__STEP_EXPECT is off (default). While the switch is on, bounds are checked by StepExpectation::validate and the expectation is stored in ctx.pending_expect for the next execute. The check runs before the step key is reserved, so a refusal takes none. ZDR is allowed: the verdict is returned on the observation, and receipts are skipped under ZDR as before. The WebSocket action path does not accept expect. openapi.yaml and ERROR_REASONS.md were updated, with CRLF kept in ERROR_REASONS.md. Verification still to do: compile quarry-edge, and run the ObservationContext and ProofBundle literal check across crates (both were already consistent with Q4-E4.2-1a and 1b at the time of this unit).
- **Q4-E4.3-043d2**: Q4-E4.3-043d2 (execution-core consumer half of the served provider catalog) built on quarry-w45 in integration_tools.rs, code only and not compiled. Behind EXECUTION_CORE_SERVED_CATALOG (literal true, default off), execution-core admits integration-corev2's /api/v1/actions/catalog. Served rows add operations the static CATALOG lacks; an operation both name is a write when either says so, so a served read never lowers a static write; an operation neither names is still gated. The copy is refreshed from list_provider_actions at most once per 10 minutes and keeps its previous copy on failure. ZDR: metadata only, nothing persisted, no ZDR skip needed. Off-state behaviour is unchanged.
- **Q4-E4.3-056a1**: Q4-E4.3-056a1 (2026-11-10): drive.file.content text reads in the Model Plane (mp-contracts provider_reads and execution-core integration_tools) now decode the executor's content_base64 and read mime_type, so Drive and OneDrive text files reach the model instead of unsupported_content. The decode is standard base64 with optional padding, whitespace ignored, UTF-8 only, and an executor truncated flag gives too_large. No new switch: the reads stay behind PROVIDER_READ_TOOL_ENABLED and CONNECTOR_ASK_READS_ENABLED, and ZDR is unchanged. The content-versus-content_base64 gap in the 2026-11-07 WS23-S7 entry is closed; moreAvailable, the per-source field gaps and execution-core's duplicate copy stay open under other units.
- **Q4-E4.3-056a2**: Q4-E4.3-056a2 (legacy execute_provider_action): github contents.get and readme.get, and notion page.content, are catalogued reads, so they run ungated under ask. Content reads (drive.file.content, contents.get, readme.get, page.content, mail.messages.get) render at READ_CONTENT_RESULT_CHARS (12,000) instead of 2,000, and the drive and GitHub base64 bodies are decoded to text in the rendered result, with non-UTF-8 bodies shown as [binary, N bytes]. Writes and other reads keep 2,000. No switch. Open items: runtime_loop/agent.rs clips a tool outcome at MAX_TOOL_CONTEXT_CHARS (8,000) before it reaches the model, so the model sees 8,000 characters of such a result until that cap is widened, which needs agent.rs and is outside this unit's files; gmail.messages.get is not in the cap list, although it returns decoded bodyText.

Lead fixes after the compile check:

- ChatMessages.tsx: a temporary (zero-retention) chat no longer mounts the crawl-plan approval card (new CrawlPlanCardAllowed context around the answer content, false when temporaryChat); the writer left that open for the owner, but a click would start a crawl that writes into the knowledge base from a zero-retention chat. The PDF export and read-only/foreign-origin threads still mount the card (follow-up).

| Q4-E4.3-056f | E4.3 Track A F-t2-056 (1) document tool wiring (plan 3.4 E4.3) | built | Built Q4-E4.3-056f in C:/dev/quarry-w45 as code only; nothing was compiled or tested, and rustfmt --check shows no diff in any hunk I wrote. read_provider_action now accepts document_url in the Do loop (execution-core) and the Ask loop (model-gateway), behind PROVIDER_READ_DOCUMENT_URL: literal true only, default off in both services, compose and the flag registry. The shared contract in mp-contracts gained DocumentUrlError, resolve_read_request, the env constant and parser, and a with_document_url flag on read_tool_parameters_json. Google drive.file.content is served; GitHub and Notion links are refused with an execute_provider_action pointer; an explicit operation must match the link. A link is only parsed, never fetched, and it cannot choose a connection; ZDR and Space are unchanged. The lead should compile and test mp-contracts, execution-core and model-gateway. | - Deviations: Schema is switch-gated: read_tool_parameters_json now takes with_document_url: bool, and the document_url property, the anyOf and the connection-only required list appear only when the switch is on. A plain edit would have changed the model-facing schema while the switch is off, so the off-state schema is byte-identical to today.; The off-state refusal is an explicit check (DOCUMENT_URL_NOT_SWITCHED_ON in both loops) rather than deny_unknown_fields: serde cannot drop a field conditionally, and the field must be deserialised for the on-state. deny_unknown_fields still rejects every other unknown field in execution-core. The Ask loop ignored unknown fields before, so that refusal is new there.; Edited runtime_loop/agent.rs, which is not in the spec's file list. read_provider_action_def now calls the contract's schema function (its inline copy was identical with the switch off), because otherwise the Do loop could never offer document_url. connector_operation, connector_call_is_read, connector_counts_note and connector_step_note now take tool_name, and for read_provider_action only a link-only read is labelled with the operation its link resolves to. Without that the step record would say op=invalid and count the read as a write.; Known limit: resolve_read_request cannot see the selected connection's provider. A Google link on a Microsoft connection is sent as drive.file.content with fileId, while the Microsoft form takes itemId, so the executor should refuse it. No scope widens. The spec signature has no provider argument, so no check was added.; The Ask loop does not offer execute_provider_action, so the pointer in the Refused text is not actionable there. The spec's single message was kept.; An empty operation with no link now reports 'requires operation, or document_url'. The Do loop previously reported unknown_operation for an empty string.; Test call sites were updated only where the signature changed (the mp-contracts schema test, and the model-gateway tool_loop and provider_read_contract tests). Their assertions still expect the switch-off state. No new tests were written.; Not changed, outside this unit: apps/Model Plane/scripts/seed_tool_knowledge.sql describes the read in prose without document_url. | Q5-S1b | E5.1 F-t2-069 step 1, W5-ORCH2 s5 (W5-S1b), D-W5-5; plan s3.4 E5.1 Scope | built | I made all four spec parts in browser.rs. | Q5-S2a | E5.2 F-t2-074 (JWKS stale-if-error), W5-ORCH2 s2 W5-S2; plan E5.2 Scope | built | Built the stale-if-error window for the quarry-edge JWKS cache, switched by AUTH_CORE_JWKS_STALE_IF_ERROR_SECS (default 0 = off, clamped to 3600). The env var is read on every call, the same way jwks_ttl() reads its own. fetch_jwks now returns a typed JwksFetchError, either Transient (connect error, timeout, 5xx, unparseable body) or Rejected (any 4xx or other non-success status, or a config error). It uses a reqwest client with a 5 s timeout and checks the HTTP status before parsing. When a refresh fails, get_jwks serves the cached set only if all of these hold: the error is Transient, the window is above 0, the refresh was not forced (so an unknown kid never gets a stale answer), and the set is younger than ttl+window. It then logs warn! with age_secs and window_secs and does not touch fetched_at, so the window cannot extend itself. A successful fetch always replaces the whole set, an empty one included. Callers still get a 500 when no stale serve applies, and the exp, aud and algorithm checks are unchanged. The spec asked for inline tests, so I added them to the existing tests module: window clamp, status classification, the stale-serve decision, 503 inside the window (fetched_at not moved), outside the window, window off, 403/404, forced refresh, and an empty JWKS replacing the cache. I also added the new env var to clear_env. Nothing was compiled or run. | - Deviations: The spec has two error kinds. An unset AUTH_CORE_JWKS_URL, a failure to build the reqwest client, and non-4xx/non-5xx statuses (such as a 3xx that was not followed) are classed as Rejected, the conservative choice, so they never serve stale.; The inline tests went into the existing tests module of auth.rs, as the spec asked. No new test file was created. The tests were not run.; I added the new env var to the module header's Environment list. The README/ops doc rows are left to Q5-S2c and the compose passthrough to Q5-S2b, as the spec says. |

| Q4-E4.4b-driver | E4.4-b 'route profile_id through ProfileStore (quarry-browser session.rs)'; plan 3.4 E4.4 scope; chromiumoxide.rs load_snapshot (line ~1920) and release path (l | built | Added the read-only profile pin to the Quarry browser driver layer. BrowserDriverCapabilities gains the additive profile_pin flag (false on the wire when absent) and BrowserDriver gains set_profile_pin(session, read_only), which defaults to unsupported, so remote providers fail closed. The chromiumoxide driver stores a per-session read-only flag: the pin is refused once a page is open or when there is no profile store, the capability is advertised only with a store, and a read-only session saves nothing on release (capture v1 and v2) or through the origin journal and pagehide flush, logging profile_save_skipped_read_only. It covers the capture v1 save and the v2 save, which is reached only through it. enable_profile_journal returns None for a read-only session, which covers the origin journal and the pagehide flush.; docs/openapi.yaml: BrowserDriverCapabilities gained a profile_pin property with reported-only wording, like trusted_input. Agent run responses serialize this schema and the public-response-field rule applies. This file was not in the spec's list; the lead can drop the property if unwanted.; A pinned lease keeps persist_profile true because load_snapshot reads only a persisting lease. The save is blocked by the session's read-only flag, not by the lease. The persist_profile doc in quarry-core/src/lease.rs was left as is because that file is outside this unit.; The older wire-test JSON in lib.rs gained "profile_pin": false so its only-difference assertion still holds; the oldest shape and the additive loop gained profile_pin.; Spec line numbers had drifted (set_page_emulation is near line 5134 of chromiumoxide.rs; the wire tests are near lines 1933-2002 of lib.rs). The real code was followed.; No driver-side ZDR refusal was added: the read-only restore neither persists nor forwards content. K=1 therefore holds per instance, and the memo's memory-budget wording covers both.; Added a fact the spec did not name: agent runs use only the local driver (agent_driver is fixed at main.rs:1777-1781), so no remote driver is reachable from an agent run today.; Found a status conflict for the ledger: escalation.rs:34 sets BUILD_DEFAULT to Honest and W4-ORCH2 section 4 says ADR 5 part (a) is decided, but W4-BLOCKERS-RANKED rank 6 still says 'no record'. Reported in the memo, not resolved.; The ADR-10 decision row (plan line 396) lists F-t2-058 as a gate, which is broader than the E4.4 recommendation (tiers only). The memo reads it as the tier part and asks that the row say so.; Section order: the memo's corrections section (section 4) sits before the decision (section 5), so the memo closes on the decision and the blocked list as the spec asks.; Did not edit docs/DRIVER_MATRIX.md (it still presents Kernel and Browserless as a working fallback and credits Kernel with VM isolation), any plan doc, the ledger, or .plan-work. Those files are outside this unit's file list or forbidden by the hard rules; the needed corrections are listed in memo section 4. |
| Q5-F1a | W5-ORCH2 FENCE-1 / D-W5-4 option (a); adapters/desktop-browser/index.mjs:246-300, page.mjs:41-62 | built | Consent dismissal in the desktop browser adapter is now off by default and reject-only. startRun({ consentReject }) opts one run in (only boolean true counts, stored as consentRejectRun and cleared in endRun and dispose); dismissOptionalCookies returns at once otherwise, so no banner is clicked by default. For an opted-in run, consentChoice returns only reject-labelled controls (the customise-or-reject fallback is removed), and a completed reject click pushes a host-only receipt, publishes it as a 'receipt' event, and returns it as `receipts` on the next snapshot. main.mjs and preload.cjs pass the options through. Naming conflict: the Q5-F1d spec reads the snapshot field as `consentReceipts`, while this unit writes `receipts` as its own spec says, so one name must be chosen before the SPA wiring lands. Syntax-checked with node --check and LF endings confirmed; no tests or builds run (CODE ONLY). | - Deviations: Receipts are one queue per adapter, drained by each snapshot() and not reset per run. A receipt pushed by a perform that then throws is returned on the next snapshot instead of being dropped; it is also published live. This is my reading of 'per-run'.; Added a pre-click guard (choice.reject !== true) in tryCookieOptOut and replaced the post-click 'if (choice.reject) return; break;' with 'return;', so a non-reject control is never clicked even if page.mjs regresses. Cursor and click mechanics are unchanged.; A receipt is recorded only when the click completed (release at the point with clickCount 1), not for an aborted press. Matches 'successful reject click'.; dispose() also clears consentRejectRun. Not in the spec; invariant hygiene only.; Cross-unit conflict, not resolved here: Q5-F1d names the snapshot field consentReceipts. This unit uses receipts, per its own spec. Either rename the two occurrences in index.mjs or have Q5-F1d read receipts. |

Owner decisions taken at the recommended option (dark code):

- **Q4-E4.2-2a**: unique-match-or-nothing; no positional fallback
- **Q4-E4.2-2b**: typed text always becomes a slot; sensitive-approval actions are not recordable in v1
- **Q4-E4.2-3j**: erase with the org; owner-scoped erasure deferred like skills
- **Q4-E4.7-ADR7**: ADR 7 note recorded at the plan recommendation: park MCP, delete CLI and n8n, SDK waiters only on demand
- **Q5-F1b**: Org policy key = new catalogue ability browser.consent.reject, baseline disabled, unlockable by an org admin
- **Q4-E4.7-B8a**: Document rather than allowlist: these are live public or service-principal routes (plan: extend coverage to agent routes)
- **Q4-E4.7-B8c**: Document public routes; keep hidden (fleets) and non-REST (graphql) entries allowlisted
- **Q4-E4.3-056e**: Resolver is a pure helper; connection choice stays with the person's selected connections
- **Q4-E4.3-056f**: Document tool = optional document_url on read_provider_action (no new tool id, so no capability-core migration)
- **Q5-S1b**: D-W5-5 = Content-Security-Policy: sandbox on application/pdf artifact responses
- **Q4-E4.3-LEDGER**: B2(c) taken at the recommended option (integration-corev2 serves the catalog)
- **Q4-E4.7-B8b**: Document the routes (decision 3.13: internal routes live only in openapi-internal.yaml); wire the two guards into CI
- **Q5-S2a**: Window default off (0); bounded at 3600 s; transient failures only
- **Q4-E4.4b-contract**: Recommended option: a pinned scrape is a restore-only, never-cached, browser-only request; unknown profile ids are a 404 (not created), decided in plan 3.4 E4.4 ('personalised and bypasses the cache')
- **Q4-E4.4a2-accept-language**: D-W5-2 taken at its recommendation: no user-agent change here; Accept-Language is a header derived from the caller's locale, not an identity change
- **Q4-E4.4a2-product-token**: D-W5-1 string QuarryBot/2.0 (+https://verevon.io/bot) and D-W5-2 'append the token to the Chromium UA, never a non-Chromium identity' both taken at their recommendations
- **Q4-E4.4b-driver**: Restore-only: a scrape never writes a profile (a hostile page could plant cookies in a user's saved profile)
- **Q4-E4.5-adr10-memo**: ADR-10 recommended option (A) K=1, pool only after a measured memory budget, no isolation claim
- **Q5-F1a**: D-W5-4 = option (a) without the 'customise' fallback click; org key default off; receipt per dismissal

Ledger notes (move into the decision ledger when a unit is proven):

- **Q4-E4.2-2a**: Q4-E4.2-2a built: ResolvedTargetProof.canonical_locator (optional, omitted when None) is filled by `canonical_locator_for` in quarry-runtime observation.rs. Candidates are tried in order TestId > Role+name > Label > Placeholder > Text, all exact. The rule is unique-match-or-nothing with no Nth fallback. Values are rejected if they are empty, over 120 bytes, carry a [redacted marker, contain an @, or contain a run of 6 or more digits. Child-frame targets get None. Nothing reads the field yet; the 2b v2 compile will read it and fail closed with TARGET_REPAIR_REQUIRED when it is absent. No wire or behaviour change, no switch, not compiled.
- **Q4-E4.2-2b**: Q4-E4.2-2b: BrowserProcedure v2 compiler built dark behind QUARRY_EDGE__PROCEDURE_CANONICAL (default off). compile_procedure_v2, compare_replay_canonical and the v2 quality checks have no edge caller yet; the receipts routes still call the v1 functions, and the edge must gate v2 on procedure_canonical_enabled() when it is wired. It depends on Q4-E4.2-2a's ResolvedTargetProof.canonical_locator, which exists only in the working tree (quarry-core contracts.rs and quarry-runtime observation.rs, uncommitted), so 2a must be committed with or before this unit. docs/RUNBOOKS.md line 1665 still describes v1-only compare_replay behaviour and needs a v2 note when wired. Stored v2 recipes hold no typed values, approval grants or positional locators. ZDR is unchanged: no receipts exist for ZDR runs, so nothing compiles.
- **Q4-E4.2-2c**: Q4-E4.2-2c built, code only, not compiled or tested. compile_run_procedure takes `canonical`: while QUARRY_EDGE__PROCEDURE_CANONICAL is off it answers 400 procedure_canonical_disabled before reading receipts; otherwise it compiles through compile_procedure_v2 and the existing driver_err maps TARGET_REPAIR_REQUIRED to 409 and UNSUPPORTED to 501. replay_check takes `prefix`: it answers 400 prefix_needs_v2 on a version 1 procedure, compares version 2 bodies with compare_replay_canonical, and keeps compare_replay for version 1. The route set is unchanged. openapi.yaml documents both fields and the new answers, and ERROR_REASONS.md has a new CRLF section for the refusals. Open question for the lead: replay_check does not read the switch, so a version 2 body is compared with the switch off.
- **Q4-E4.2-3j**: Q4-E4.2-3j (session-core org erasure of saved procedures) is built in quarry-w45, in gdpr.rs only. The org purge transaction now removes procedure_tags, procedure_versions and procedures (capability-core migration 0039, same session_core database), guarded by to_regclass on procedure_versions so a deployment without the migration still erases. A partly applied migration behaves differently by state: procedure_versions present with procedure_tags or procedures missing fails the erasure loudly, while procedures present without procedure_versions would skip silently. Erasure is org-scoped only; the per-owner (owner_user_id) path stays deferred with skills under register D1-643. No ZDR change, since recipes are org configuration and not conversation content. Not compiled or tested in this unit; the batch compile should cover the new PurgeSummary fields and the extended source-scan test.

Decisions: OpenAPI is the external contract (route-coverage and scrape-parity tests run in CI at quarry-v2-ci.yml:283-284; the internal-spec guards are added by Q4-E4.7-B8b); MCP parked (revisit only on an owner decision that third-party agent access is a goal); CLI and n8n deleted from scope; SDK wait helpers only after WS15 and an external SDK consumer; F-t2-065 cached extraction profiles parked behind ADR 6, the WS16 model grant and cumulative cost enforcement. OSS_PARITY_BACKLOG.md rows 30 and 112-114 annotated with the decision record and the applied date 2026-11-11. The ledger should record ADR 7 as proposed until the owner ratifies it.
- **Q5-F1b**: Q5-F1b (2026-11-11, branch quarry-w45): the Quarry web-policy catalogue gains the ability browser.consent.reject (applies to all, baseline disabled, unlockable, not ZDR-forced, not reserved, since W5-FENCE-1). It is the org policy key for D-W5-4(a), and its consumer is the desktop browser adapter's consentReject run option, fed by the SPA. The golden web-policy-catalogue.v1.json and its pin are now b6c0a104c274c5820a95dc60cb5a11a1e95b046611460a40476698fe57665abb, applied by hand because cargo was not run, and the zdr-contract-sync GOLDENS row matches. Three things are open before this is useful. The handler sends the query to the Model Plane /v1/invoke through ModelPlaneInvokeRequest, which has no zdr field, so no ZDR posture reaches the model. (2) The images, videos and similar routes build error codes with format!("{:?}").to_uppercase() (search_routes.rs vertical_error_response and similar), so they return UPSTREAMBLOCKED or RATELIMITED rather than the underscore wire names that QuarryError::wire_name() gives. - **Q5-S2a**: Q5-S2a built (code only, not compiled): quarry-edge auth.rs now has a bounded JWKS stale-if-error window, switched by AUTH_CORE_JWKS_STALE_IF_ERROR_SECS (default 0 = off, clamped to 3600, read on every call). fetch_jwks returns JwksFetchError::{Transient, Rejected}, uses a 5 s client timeout and checks the status before parsing. Stale is served only on a Transient failure, a non-forced refresh, and age < ttl+window measured from the last successful fetch. fetched_at is never moved on a stale serve. A 4xx or an unknown-kid forced refresh never serves stale. Any successful fetch, an empty JWKS included, replaces the whole cache. Inline tests were added to the auth.rs tests module. Security review is required before the switch is set. Docs follow in Q5-S2c and the compose passthrough in Q5-S2b.

Before this, they were lenient unknown_field warnings that the crawl ignored silently. The change is the UNSUPPORTED_KEYS list in crawl_scrape.rs (16 to 21 entries). ResolvedScrapeOptions.profile_id holds the canonical id. The schema goldens and openapi were edited by hand and need regeneration.
- **Q4-E4.4a2-accept-language**: Q4-E4.4a2 (Accept-Language per page) is code-complete and dark, but not compiled or run. QUARRY_EDGE__PAGE_ACCEPT_LANGUAGE (off) works together with QUARRY_EDGE__PER_PAGE_LOCALE: when both are on, a scrape with a locale sets PageEmulation.send_accept_language. The Chromium driver then sends Network.setExtraHTTPHeaders with only Accept-Language, derived by quarry_core::page_emulation::accept_language_for from the checked locale (nb-NO -> "nb-NO,nb;q=0.9", de -> "de", max 64 characters). It is applied once at tab open and never in response to a status. The UA is unchanged (D-W5-2, ADR-5 rules 1 and 3), and navigator.languages is unchanged too. The header also reaches subresources. A failure is page_emulation_failed with field accept_language. The flag is part of the serialized emulation, so it is part of the options key and the effective_options echo. There is no ZDR change. The effective-scrape-options schema golden needs regenerating (UPDATE_SCHEMAS=1), and the RUNBOOKS E4.4-a section has a new Accept-Language paragraph.
- **Q4-E4.4a2-product-token**: Q4-E4.4a2-product-token built dark behind QUARRY_BROWSER__PRODUCT_TOKEN (default off, read once in quarry-browser chromiumoxide.rs). Local Chromium tabs keep the native UA from Browser.getVersion and append quarry_core::identity::HONEST_USER_AGENT through Emulation.setUserAgentOverride (userAgent only). This is installed at tab open from static config, never from a block or challenge signal. On failure it only warns (`browser_identity_override_failed`, closed classes). Profile restore strips a trailing token before setting ua_changed. Cloud drivers are untouched. The plan's dependency order was wrong: Q5-S3a (the quarry_core::identity module) sits in QB10 after this QB09 unit, so this unit created identity.rs with Q5-S3a's full non-test surface plus append_product_token and strip_product_token. Q5-S3a now only owes its inline tests. Owed: a live #[ignore] test checking that Sec-CH-UA and navigator.userAgentData survive the override; if Chromium drops them, build userAgentMetadata from Browser.getVersion. Nothing has been compiled yet.
- **Q4-E4.4b-driver**: Q4-E4.4b-driver (code only, quarry-w45, not compiled): the browser driver can restore a stored profile read-only into a session pinned to it. BrowserDriverCapabilities.profile_pin (additive, true only with a profile store) and BrowserDriver::set_profile_pin(session, read_only) (default refused) are added. The chromiumoxide driver refuses the pin once a page is open, saves nothing for a read-only session (release v1 and v2 saves, and the origin journal and pagehide flush; closed log event profile_save_skipped_read_only), and advertises the capability. RenderHints.profile_pin routes through BrowserDriverAdapter::do_fetch: refused before the pool with 501 browser_capability_unavailable when the driver lacks the capability; otherwise an isolated lease with the pinned profile and persist_profile true, pinned read-only before the egress policy and first navigation. It recommends option A (one browser process per local driver instance, no isolation claim, no pool until a measured memory budget exists), with a per-org sidecar design (C) before any remote backend (D). As a result, an open-web run stays DNS-guarded on every navigation path even when the engine allows private hosts. Each site keeps its existing error mapping and release-on-error. `validate_navigation` now has only test callers (agent_routes/tests.rs and admission tests). It is kept but marked `#[cfg(test)]` with a doc comment, which avoids a dead-code warning. | - Deviations: Spec allowed deriving the nav policy from checkpoint policy_mode in lifecycle.rs if not in scope; it was already in scope (`nav_policy` local built by human_browse::admit_start and verified by resume_policy_matches), so that is used directly.; admission.rs was also touched (outside the spec's file list) to put `#[cfg(test)]` on `validate_navigation`, as the spec allows, since it has no non-test caller left. |

The helper kept_tab_id(&QuarryError) -> Option<&str> is pub(super) in tabs.rs and used by both files. No switch, proto, route, response-shape or openapi change. ZDR holds: only ids and classes are stored and no URL is added to any detail. rustfmt --check reports no parse errors on either file; nothing was compiled or tested. | - Deviations: new_tab bookkeeping is best effort rather than propagated as the success path does: if list_tabs or the timeline write fails after a kept tab, the failure is logged by error code and the Timeout is still returned, so the caller can still act on tab_id. | Q4-E4.2-3e | plan 3.4 E4.2 step 3 runner proposes each stored step and runs compare_replay first; quarry_agent.rs wire types | built | Built all four parts of Q4-E4.2-3e in the execution-core Quarry client. WireObservation reads `action_outcome` leniently (status and reason_code only, never serialised back), and WireSnapshotTarget reads tag, placeholder, test_id and the fingerprint id as read-only fields that are never serialised, so the chat-facing tool output is unchanged. Every Quarry field name and route the spec names matches Quarry's source, so no spec detail needed adapting; nothing was compiled, and rustfmt --check parsed both edited files, with its remaining diffs being pre-existing formatting in untouched code. | - Deviations: WireSnapshotTarget's four new fields (tag, placeholder, test_id, fingerprint) use #[serde(default, skip_serializing)] rather than the spec's #[serde(default)] alone. tool_bridge/mod.rs serialises the whole WireObservation into the chat-facing tool output, so serialised fields would add keys (nulls included) to every browser step's output. This follows the existing redaction/warnings/settle_ms/action_outcome rule.; WireFingerprintId.fingerprint_id carries #[serde(default)] so a fingerprint object without an id reads as an empty id instead of failing the whole observation parse. Quarry always sends the id; the type is still String.; browser_agent/browser_risk.rs (not in the unit's file list) got `..WireSnapshotTarget::default()` on its one WireSnapshotTarget struct literal in the test helper wire_page, required by the struct-literal rule once fields were added.; The spec cites 7 WireObservation literals. Only 3 exist (quarry_agent.rs test observation_from_wire_maps_fields_and_blocked, browser_agent.rs test page(), browser_risk.rs test wire_page), and all already use ..Default, so none was changed.; replay_check reuses the existing send_with_auth_retry helper (bearer, x-quarry-org, with_request_id, single 401 retry, same as step) instead of an inline copy of the loop; behaviour is identical.; Spec line numbers had drifted (StepRequest is at 464, WireSnapshotTarget at 1223, StepParams at 2185, step() at 2343); the edits were placed by content, not by line number. |
| Q4-E4.2-3f | plan 3.4 E4.2 step 3 'deterministic planner mode ... proposes each stored step under a fresh grant' | built | Added the pure saved-recipe module browser_agent/recipe.rs (new, LF) and declared it with `pub mod recipe;` in browser_agent.rs, after `procedure`. It parses a version 2 procedure (1..=100 steps, the 12-action vocabulary, unsupported actions refused by step number), binds text slots (secret slots refused, 2000-char cap, no control characters), and resolves semantic locators with the same rules as Quarry's semantic_matches (nth never matches). proposal_for_step turns one step into a BrowserAction with action_id recipe-<n>, reason "recipe step <n>", the matched ref and frame, and a check_json carrying the fresh snapshot_id/generation (plus the bound text for typing steps); None or Ambiguous gives TargetRepairRequired. Inline unit tests are included but were not run, and rustfmt --check passes on the new file. | - Deviations: Recipe::parse also refuses inconsistent slot wiring as Malformed: a typing step with no slot, a slot its named step does not own, duplicate step numbers, or duplicate slot names. The spec did not list this; it is fail-closed.; A semantic step whose target.locator differs from the action's locator is refused as Malformed. Resolution and check_json both use the action's locator.; resolve_locator returns LocatorMatch::None, not One, when the single match has an empty ref_id, because it cannot be addressed.; Refusal enums carry more variants than the spec named: RecipeRefusal::{Malformed, UnsupportedVersion, StepCount, SlotUnbound, UnknownSlot, SecretSlot, SlotValueInvalid} and StepRefusal::{Malformed, UnsupportedAction, SlotUnbound}. Both have a closed as_str().; RecipeStep.expected_outcome is a closed enum RecipeOutcome (verified, failed, unknown; any other value reads as unknown). The spec left its type open.; proposal_for_step copies the matched target's frame_id into BrowserTargetRef, so a child-frame target goes out as a frame ref action. Needed to keep the frame contract; the spec only said 'binds from the resolved ref'. |
| Q4-E4.2-3g | plan 3.4 E4.2 step 3 'runs compare_replay first, and hands back to the planner or user on mismatch' | built | Built the Q4-E4.2-3g replay planner in execution-core. browser_agent/recipe_planner.rs (new) holds RecipePlanner, the ReplayChecker trait and QuarryReplayChecker over QuarryAgentClient::replay_check; it proposes each stored step against the latest snapshot, checks it with Quarry before dispatch, and hands back on any mismatch, unverified outcome, or unanswered proposal. llm_planner.rs adds two default methods to ActionPlanner (step_expect, note_wire_observation) that change nothing for other planners. browser_agent.rs wires them in: dispatch_step takes an expect that only leased runs send, the main call passes it and notes each successful observation, and both probe sites pass None. No switch is added here, since 3h selects the planner. Nothing was compiled and no tests were written (CODE ONLY); rustfmt --check parses all three files with no errors, and the new file is rustfmt-clean. | - Deviations: step_expect and note_wire_observation match the dispatched action by content (action type, selector, trimmed value, url, target), not by action_id. decide_next_action overwrites the planner's action_id with the loop's act_NNNN id before dispatch, so a recipe-N key would never match.; Added halts beyond the spec's list, all hand-back: a proposal the loop does not answer with an observation halts the recipe at the next plan_next (covers admission and human rejections, takeover give-back, and recoverable failures); a widened verb halts when ctx.config.widened_actions is off, because admission would otherwise read it as observe.; The proposed target's top-level frame id is cleared to match observation_from_wire. Without it, bind_observed_target rejects every root-frame semantic step as unobserved_target. recipe.rs (3f) was not edited.; RecipePlanner::new also takes the stored procedure JSON, because check() needs the procedure as Quarry holds it and BoundRecipe keeps only the parsed Recipe.; ReplayChecker::check takes (procedure, actions, prefix). QuarryReplayChecker wraps the client with org_id and request_id, because replay_check needs the org and the trait cannot carry it.; The Ok-arm note is written as `if let Some(p) = planner { p.note_wire_observation(..) }` rather than planner.map(..), which would discard an unused Option<()>. The effect is the same.; Outcome rule: verified advances, failed halts, and unknown, another status string, or an absent action_outcome halts only on a step with an expect or verified at compile; otherwise it advances. An absent outcome is read as unknown, which is stricter than the spec's literal 'otherwise advance'.; Halt texts: 'recipe step <n> needs repair' for any refusal, replay mismatch, or replay error; 'recipe step <n> did not verify' for an outcome halt, since the spec named no text for that case. |

| Q2-WS15.S4b | Plan 3.2 WS15.S4 rows schedule_runs.go updateRuntime and schedules.go reconcileWith; SCHEDULES.md Known gap 4 | built | Built PUT /v1/internal/schedules/{id}/runtime. updateScheduleRuntime in schedule_runs.go decodes a three-field body (strict, 4 KiB cap), refuses a missing or negative skipped_overlap_count and any timestamp outside UTC years 2000 to 2200 with 400, and calls SchedulesStore.UpdateRuntime, answering 404 for a missing schedule and 204 on success. The spec's int64 >= 0 was read as required so that a PUT cannot silently reset the stored count to 0.; The body decodes with DisallowUnknownFields, so a key outside last_run_at, next_run_at and skipped_overlap_count (org_id, params, ...) is a 400 rather than ignored, to honour 'never accepts org or params'. It is now 9, otherwise that test fails. This file was not in the spec's file list.; Name: the handler is updateScheduleRuntime as the spec says; the build plan's WS15.S4 table calls it updateRuntime. I updated it in place (no new test file) and renamed it to a_dynamic_client_that_cannot_mint_or_an_unauthenticated_client_sends_nothing. It now expects LaneUnavailable, one refused mint, an empty org rejected without calling the provider, a client with no credential still LaneNotGranted, and nothing sent.; CONTRACTS.md 'The model lane' paragraph said the minted path was not built. | Q2-WS16.S6a | Plan 3.2 WS16.S6 (~2981) extract_routes.rs row: /v1/extract uses the same engine, buffer_unordered concurrency, per-item ai report | built | The model arm of /v1/extract no longer builds a per-request AiFormatRunner on the old /v1/invoke json path. It now runs on the shared AiScrapeEngine over /v1/ai/chat, which comes from the new `pub(crate) fn engine_for_request(state)` in ai_formats.rs (same settings, same cache, same minted or dev lane, same process semaphore). Each source gets one AiSpec::Json run: the schema is checked with parse_ai_object (a bad schema is a 400 before any fetch), the prompt is the instruction, and a prompt-only request uses {"type":"object"}. Sources run through futures::stream::iter(..).buffer_unordered(settings().concurrency) and are sorted back into `urls` order. A shared stop flag stops scheduling new fetches or model calls after the first 429 (driver or MODEL_RATE_LIMITED, which keeps the Model Plane retry_after) or after a lane refusal (still the 501 case 3 when no source has been extracted). The engine deadline is started + budget (50 s). Every item now carries `ai` (the json AiFormatReport), and `data` appears only when the answer is schema-valid. Unchanged: the 501 texts, the web-policy gate, ZDR (no engine is built, each source ZDR_UNAVAILABLE with ai skipped), the privacy block, quarry.extract.url metering. New: a 403 when a service principal without extract:ai sends a schema or prompt. openapi.yaml (ExtractItem.ai, data/error/schema_valid descriptions, a 403 response, the 400 text, the route description) and a new CONTRACTS.md section document all of this. | - Deviations: A schema answer that is still invalid after the engine's repair is now status `error` (error text starts `SCHEMA_INVALID:`), with schema_valid false, schema_issues as `path: code` lines and no `data`. Before, it was status `ok` with the invalid data. Why: the engine never returns invalid output, and the spec says `data` is schema-valid or absent. CONTRACTS and openapi now say this.; Added a 403 FORBIDDEN for a service principal without `extract:ai` that sends a schema or prompt. The spec lists the extract:ai scope check under 'Keep', but /v1/extract had no such check (only extract:read at the router), so this is new. It follows the scrape route's check_principal rule, checked inline because check_principal's details point at `formats[i]`.; A lane refusal also stops scheduling once a source has been extracted. The refused source and any unread ones get a per-source `MODEL_UNAVAILABLE:` error instead of each spending its own model call. With concurrency, more than one page may be fetched before the stop flag is seen (before, exactly one).; Kept REQUEST_BUDGET = 50 s (the router's cut is 60 s) and passed it as the engine deadline. Model-arm error texts are new constants that start with the AiCode name. |
| Q2-WS10.S4a | plan 3.2 WS10 Slice WS10.S4 detail, rows quarry-core trace.rs / event.rs / lib.rs / pkg/quarrycontracts/event.go (~1925-1930) | built | I added a new quarry-core trace module (`pub mod trace;`). A user token can only reach Agent (otherwise User). A service token gets its parsed hint, otherwise Api.; Inline #[cfg(test)] tests were added inside the new trace.rs; no separate test file was created and nothing was run.; gofmt -l flags event.go for a constant-alignment issue that is already in HEAD. | Q2-WS10.S4b | plan 3.2 WS10 Slice WS10.S4 detail, row quarry-runtime trace_projection.rs and 'Payload allowlists' (~1931, ~1938); MP-ASKS.md Lane D producer contract | built | I added a new pure module, quarry-runtime `trace_projection.rs`. It holds `PublishCtx`, `model_event_type` and `to_model_envelope`, plus per-type payload allowlists, `MAX_PAYLOAD_BYTES = 4096`, `MAX_STRING_CHARS = 256` and a re-export of `is_agent_class` (named in the plan row) from `nats_event_bus`. Mapping denies by default (anything not listed falls to `_ => None`): AgentStarted and BrowserSessionOpened map to BROWSER_SESSION_OPENED; AgentCompleted, AgentFailed, AgentExpired and BrowserSessionClosed map to BROWSER_SESSION_CLOSED; the other five new types map 1:1. `to_model_envelope` returns None for a ZDR run, an invalid `model_run_id`, an empty or over-long org id, seq 0, or an unmapped type. The envelope always writes `"zdr": false` and uses the bare ULID event id, producer `quarry-edge`, `correlation_id` and `run_id` both set to the Model run id, idempotency key `quarry:<browser_run_id>:<seq>`, resource ref `browser_run/<id>` and `producer_seq` set to seq; origin, agent, request_id and causation_id come from `event.trace`, and invalid tokens are dropped. Payloads keep only allowlisted scalar keys, add `browser_run_id` from the context, and reduce `url_origin` and `origin` to scheme://host[:port]. A payload over 4096 bytes becomes `{truncated:true, browser_run_id}`. lib.rs adds `pub mod trace_projection` and re-exports `model_event_type`, `to_model_envelope`, `PublishCtx` and `TRACE_MAX_PAYLOAD_BYTES`. Nothing was compiled; only `rustfmt --check` was run on the new file, and it passes. | - Deviations: The timestamp key is `ts` (RFC 3339 with millis, Z), not `occurred_at`. `ts` is a required field of `mp_events::envelope::Envelope` and the key used in the vendored golden `run-trace-envelope.golden.json`; session-core maps it to its `occurred_at` column. Emitting `occurred_at` alone would fail serde decoding (Invalid::Shape).; `causation_id` is always present: an empty string when the trace has no causation id, because Envelope requires the key. The causation id's `evt_` prefix is stripped so it is a bare ULID like `event_id`. Agent, origin and request_id are left out when absent.; Strings over 256 chars are dropped, not truncated (default deny). Nested objects, arrays and nulls are dropped too. `decision` is limited to block/summary. `reason` is limited to closed/expired/failed/cancelled; when it is missing on AgentCompleted, AgentFailed or AgentExpired, it is filled with closed, failed or expired. `url_origin` and EGRESS `origin` keep only the http(s) tuple origin.; Extra fail-closed rules not in the spec: seq 0, and an empty or over-128-char org id, both return None, matching session-core `validate_trace_envelope`.; `resource_ref` (`browser_run/<id>`) and `idempotency_key` (`quarry:<browser_run_id>:<seq>`) follow the unit spec. The Model golden instead uses `run/<model_run_id>` and the event id, so mapper test C2 must compare with those two fields excluded, or the golden or the spec needs reconciling.; BrowserSessionOpened and BrowserSessionClosed also map, in addition to AgentStarted and AgentCompleted, AgentFailed and AgentExpired. Emitters (S4d) should emit only one of each pair per run, or the trace will carry two opened/closed events.; The `TRACE_MAX_PAYLOAD_BYTES` alias is used in lib.rs re-exports, to avoid a name clash at crate root. |
| QF-11-spa-steer-held-wiring | followups.md QB02 Q3-E3.3-g3 (ChatPage.tsx does not pass onSteerHeldMessages/onResend); plan 3.3 E3.3 steer and queue | built | The gap was present when checked: ChatPage passed neither onSteerHeldMessages nor a resend handler, and ChatLiveRunPanel had no onSteerResend prop, so the Queue button and the send-as-new button stayed hidden. ChatLiveRunPanel now takes an optional onSteerResend prop and passes it to SteerInput as onResend. ChatPage adds sendHeldSteerMessages, which sends held or resent messages one at a time through submitWithWorkPeriod (the composer's own send path), stops at the first failure, and sends nothing in a foreign-origin or temporary thread; the unsent text goes back into the composer draft with an nb/en notice. The composer's own draft is restored after the sends, because a send clears it. No action contract, flag, proto, migration or golden changed, and nothing was compiled or tested. | - Deviations: The helper parameter is typed readonly Pick<SteerResend, 'content'>[] rather than readonly SteerResend[], because the spec's [{ content }] does not type-check against SteerResend, which also requires clientMessageId. The SteerResend type is still used via Pick.; The payload also carries tools: ['search'] when the page's browse toggle is on, and no tools in a conversation-only thread. The spec's enumerated payload did not name tools; this mirrors the composer's web toggle so a held message browses the way an ordinary send would. Set tools: [] to match the spec literally.; The guard also refuses when the composer's Temporary chat toggle (temporaryChat()) is on, not only when the thread is temporary, because this payload does not carry zdr and the composer sets zdr for that toggle.; A send clears the composer, so the page captures the composer draft before the held sends and restores it after them. The text not sent (the failed message and those after it) is placed ahead of that draft.; The notice is a dedicated steerNotice signal rendered in the composer strip with the existing verevon-chat-project-notice element. Any submitWithWorkPeriod call clears it, so a stale notice does not outlive the next send.; The guard runs before each send in the loop, and scopes are read per send, so a toggle flipped during an earlier send is respected. |

| Q4-062b-sseid-c | Plan 3.4 E4.6 062 (b) 'addressable run'; inventory Q4-062b-sse built the record but the chat stream never tells the client the research run id (only the researc | built | Added ChatEvent::ResearchRun (SSE event research_run, data {research_run_id, status}) in sse_events.rs, and sse.rs emits it at the begin of a recorded deep-research run (running) and at each of its three finish call sites (failed; cancelled on a client stop; the pipeline's own status). The status mapping is extracted into research_status and shared with research_finish, so the frame and the finish record use one mapping. The frame is gated by the existing MODEL_GATEWAY_RESEARCH_RUNS read, goes out through emit_and_buffer so it is buffered for resume, and is ungated by feature family. Until it exists, scripts/gen-sse-event-taxonomy.py fails with 'has NO case in the SPA stream client' (the generator raises before writing, and its --check gate in .github/workflows/sse-taxonomy.yml goes red). 'expired' is store-side and never written by this stream, so it is not in the set.; room_run.rs was edited although it is not in the spec's file list: ROOM_EVENT_POLICY gained ('research_run', Drop). The room leak guard every_taxonomy_event_has_a_policy_row requires a row for every name in docs/sse-event-taxonomy.md, and the regenerated taxonomy will list research_run. Without the row that test fails after regeneration.; The frame is a control event (family None, always emitted), which the spec did not name. The id is the only way to reach the read and cancel routes, so a client that opted into no feature family still needs it. The MODEL_GATEWAY_RESEARCH_RUNS read is the gate.; Client-cancel branch: the research_run end frame is emitted before the TurnTiming and Stopped frames and before end_resume_buffer, so it is buffered before the stream is marked ended. Its status is research_status(research.stop), which equals the status the finish below records. The finish call itself stays after the stopped frame, so the stop frame is not delayed by a session-core round trip. A refused finish would leave the record running to its deadline, as before, while the frame already said cancelled.; seq = sink.sync_cursor(seq).await is added after the running frame (before run_deep_research) and after the normal-path end frame. This is required by the RichEventSink cursor contract so pipeline frames never reuse the frame's SSE id. It is not in the spec.; The frame's data is exactly {research_run_id, status} as the spec states, without the request_id key that most control frames carry.; The payload_contract test table in sse_events.rs (VARIANTS, covered(), two samples) was updated so the exhaustive match compiles and the contract names the variant.
